# Multi-Level Work Breakdown Structure (WBS)

> **Companion design document**. This document accompanies
> [RFC-039 Compiler Architecture Refactoring](../../rfc/accepted/039-compiler-architecture.md) and
> expands the RFC's phase sequence into **independently committable and independently verifiable**
> three-level tasks.
>
> RFC-039's main text is the sole authority for phase order and acceptance gates. This document is
> responsible only for decomposing the work to an executable granularity and registering conflicts
> that have not yet been decided.

## Positioning and Scope

| Level       | Count       | Meaning                                                                                          |
| ----------- | ----------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| **Level 1** | 11 (P0–P10) | Phases, corresponding one-to-one with RFC-039's phase sequence                                   |
| **Level 2** | 73          | Task groups: work groups decomposed by phase, each able to form and archive a team independently |
| **Level 3** | 262         | Work units that can be committed and verified independently                                      |

**Reserved gaps (not reused)**: P4 has no `4.7`/`4.8`, and P8 has no `8.1`; after the LALRPOP route
was withdrawn, `8.4` / `8.5` / `8.6` were also left blank (2026-11-25). None of these three has had
a corresponding entry since the WBS was established—the headings `**4.7` / `**4.8` / `**8.1` have
zero matches in the full history of this document
(`git log -S'**4.7' -- docs/src/dev/architecture/09-execution-wbs.md` can be used to verify this),
and there are no repository-wide references either. `8.1` corresponds precisely to phase 1 of `05`
(test revival and completion): that phase was split into P1 (`1.1`–`1.3`) and `8.8`, so P8's
numbering is aligned with its phase numbers (phase 0 → `8.0`, phase 2 → `8.2`, and so on).

**Covered here**: Task decomposition, dependencies, parallelization groups, and binding of
acceptance criteria.

**Not covered here**: The design rationale for tasks (see `02`–`08`) or the rationale for phase
order (see RFC-039).

**Registration convention (effective 2026-10-12)**: Every newly registered level-2/level-3 row
**must contain two fields**—“**Owning phase**” (the P to which it truly belongs, which may differ
from its current section) and “**Blocks mainline**”; `pending scheduling` is no longer the default
state, and an unmarked task is considered **not schedulable**. Numbering must be **unique across the
entire document** (tasks on separate tracks use a track number, such as `029g.x`, and must not
occupy an `N.x` number).

**Closure registration (effective 2026-11-24)**: A checked row contains only four items—the task ID,
status, **implementation commit short hash** (7 characters), and issue number, if any—in one line:
`- [x] **3.24.1 Remaining pattern-matching work** ✅ 4d608df3 (issue #405)`. Do **not** record the
fix, criterion chain, gate values, file line numbers, test names, or review conclusions; those
belong in the commit message (`git show <short-hash>`). This table is a board, not a process
chronicle. **Do not record decision labels or restate conclusions**: “User decision A/B/C,” “Option
A,” “Option B,” “Final implementation is…” and similar statements do not belong here. The board
answers only “Is it complete?”; decisions and results are recorded in commit messages and the
RFC-039 decision register.

**Short hashes are authoritative only after pushing**: A closure row records the short hash on
`origin/dev` after the implementation commit has been pushed. The order is **push the implementation
commit first, then write the closure row** (see “Commit hash is obtained after push” in
[commit-convention](../commit-convention.md)). The closure commit itself carries no hash—a
self-reference is impossible.

**The count column contains only numbers**: The level-1/level-2/level-3 counts under “Positioning
and Scope” contain only current values; increment/decrement history (`+1 = …`) does not belong in
the table.

## Current State: Gaps Found During Decomposition

While consolidating the “Implementation Points” of eight documents into this table, **five hard gaps
in the documentation system itself** were found. All have now been resolved:

| #   | Gap                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Resolution                                                                                                                                     |
| --- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| G1  | **P5 has no implementation steps.** RFC defines P5 (checker splitting) and its acceptance criterion (C1 zero-diff), but none of the seven documents contains a section describing how to perform the split; a single `annotations.rs` entry appeared in the directory tree of `01-routing.md`, making it the only mention in the entire batch                                                                                                         | P5's steps are supplied by this document (see below)                                                                                           |
| G2  | **The three ownership points of the `include!` refactor have zero implementations.** RFC lists it under P4; `02` assigns the reference to itself; and S7 of `06` also lists it. However, the change list in `02` (6 additions + 20 modifications) **does not include `semantic_tokens.rs`**                                                                                                                                                           | The implementation steps are assigned to §P5 5.1 of this document (after P4 is complete); references in RFC and `06` now point here            |
| G3  | **Three concurrent inventories of CI scripts exist.** `RFC` lists 4 `scripts/ci/*.py` scripts; `01` lists 4 scripts + 1 `cargo test`; `07` separately lists 4 checks (all with different names); and `04` previously listed `check-synth-boundary.py` (that script was cancelled by the user on 2026-11-24). After deduplication, there are **at least 9 new scripts and no unified inventory**; `check-obligations.py` is owned jointly by P4 and P9 | See §P9 of this document for the unified inventory (10 active scripts, each owned by one phase); all other documents refer to that table       |
| G4  | **Conflict over the life or death of `precedence_inline.rs`.** `06` counts it among the “1,005 lines that must be revived,” while B6 of the same document says it “must be deleted together with the `Precedence` enum”; `05` advocates deletion                                                                                                                                                                                                      | **Revive in P1, retain in P8** (decision registration C1; Pratt is no longer to be deleted)                                                    |
| G5  | **Conflicting wording for the L2 goal**: RFC and the directory index previously said “grammar-driven syntax”                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | This has converged to a **declarative precedence table + an unambiguous language surface** ([05](05-frontend-paradigm.md) §Syntax); see P8 8.3 |

## Target Design: Phase Sequence

```
P0 仓库维护机制与代码放置规程 08 ← 新增，先立规矩
P1 复活孤儿测试并修缺陷 06 §S1
P2 建立等价性判据基线 07 全部
P3 修正确性漏洞（最小方案） 02 §S3-漏洞半部
P4 阶段契约与统一 Driver 02
P5 checker 文件内拆分 02 + 本文补齐
P6 类型表示单一化 03
P7 中间表示 SSA 化 04 } 可并行
P8 前端范式变更 05 }
P9 防反弹门禁（含统一脚本清单） 01 §防反弹 + 08 §可机检规则
P10 其余清理与状态修正 06 §S2/S3/S4/S5/S6
```

**Rationale for adding P0**: The three prohibitions—do not invent things, do not fill indefinitely,
and do not patch when a refactoring is required—constrain every action in P1–P10. The rules must be
established before work begins; otherwise, when P1 revives tests, people are already adding `mod`
declarations to `tests/mod.rs`, which is precisely the cause of the G4 conflict.

> the gate is false.

### P3 Fix Correctness Vulnerabilities (Minimal Approach)

Source: Vulnerability-fix half of §Implementation Points S3 in [02](02-stage-contract.md) ＋
**2026-10-07 Silent-Discard-Point Audit (3.4)**

> **Semantic-correction routing (D59, user decision of 2026-10-11)**: If this track or a subsequent
> phase discovers a defect for which “the old behavior does not count as success”—**A silent failure
> / B silent mismatch / C undefined behavior and uncontrolled resources** (crashes, stack
> exhaustion, silently incorrect answers)—it **may be fixed along with the refactoring**, but all
> four of the following are required: ① red before green (the red criterion must exist first and
> genuinely be red); ② do not change the externally visible behavior of programs that “previously
> compiled successfully and ran correctly” (they must not become failures, and their existing
> diagnostic codes/text/exit codes must not be changed or removed); ③ register in the same commit
> (mark the error type on the row + add a phase revision note + add an “Error type” line to the PR's
> required block); ④ regenerate the baseline in the same commit + manually review the diff. **The
> three categories D user-visible semantics / E external-contract tables / F RFC-level design
> changes must still receive a decision first. See
> [07 §Semantic-Correction Routing Criteria](07-equivalence-oracle.md) for the expanded criteria and
> A–F allowlist; the “default super-refactor + post-hoc registration” approach is not adopted** (the
> rationale is in RFC-039 D59: with no red criterion, fixes and regressions cannot be distinguished,
> and this conflicts with “no C5′ exists” and “no relaxation whatsoever”).

> **Batch (user decision of 2026-10-14)**: The four small items 3.13 / 3.14 / 3.15 / 3.16 are
> **highly related** (method binding, resolution, and inference entry point all belong to one path;
> 3.16 is another carrier of the same “named arguments” criterion—stdlib native exports). They are
> **merged into one team**—the team scope is relaxed from “one level-2 task” to “a batch of related
> small items”; batch composition, rationale, and deviations must be registered when the team
> closes.

|                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | 3.20.20 Eq/Neq permits cross-type and static-Void comparisons at compile time—investigation found that the **decision package must be presented and is awaiting a decision** (investigation and presentation completed, issue [#549](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/549)).** The investigation disproves the premise that it “can be fixed along the way as category C”: a probe matrix empirically confirms that container mismatched-Eq forms (`[1,2]==(1,2)` / `[1]==["x"]` / `[1]==[2.5]` / `(1,2)==(1,"x")`) compile successfully and **run successfully, printing false**—compile-time rejection would change the externally visible behavior of programs that previously ran successfully ⇒ D59 **category D**, so the along-the-way-fix conditions are not met; scalar cross-type / static-Void comparison / cross-type Dict comparison fall into E6007 at runtime (old behavior was not success and may be fixed along the way, but this does not constitute an independent decision point); it was also observed that **same-type** Dict==Dict also falls into E6007 at runtime (no comparison arm for Handle; recorded without decision). The sole decision point = whether mismatched-container Eq should be rejected at compile time (Option A: compile-time rejection, with a same-type static discipline on both sides of sorting comparison (the criterion body = infer_binary comparison arm)—recommended; Option B: retain runtime false and use the design process); all four parts of the complete decision package are in the comments on issue [#549](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/549). **Acceptance**: Vulnerability criteria turn green, and **intentionally removing the fix must make
them turn red again**. **Risk**: the new E4018 on the multi-file path is a breaking change.

**3.4 acceptance**: 3.4.1 / 3.4.2 must have red criteria first (changing code first is prohibited);
3.4.3 requires manual review; 3.4.4–3.4.8 use C2/C3 and the 9.2 gate in P9 as criteria. **Risk**:
the new diagnostic in 3.4.1 is a behavior change, so the corpus differential baseline and CHANGELOG
must be updated in sync.

> **Stop-the-bleed path**: P3 need not wait for all of P2. The only hard prerequisites for 3.1/3.2
> are **2.3.2 (multi-file corpus layer) + 2.4.1/2.4.3 (two red criteria)**; 2.1/2.2/2.3.1/2.3.3 (the
> IR verifier, snapshots, single-file differential, and performance baseline) can proceed in
> parallel with P3. Stopping the correctness vulnerability (`Sorted(3)` silently passing) must not
> be blocked by snapshot-infrastructure construction. 3.3 (panic to diagnostic) has no prerequisite
> and can land at any time.

### External Track RFC-029g: Remove `pub` and Automatic Binding (External Track / #399 — Ordering Constraint D56)

Source: [RFC-029g](../../rfc/accepted/029g-remove-pub-and-auto-bind.md) (accepted 2026-10-02;
implementation not started), [#399](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/399). **This track
is not a design item of the refactoring itself; it is a specification-compliance deletion forced by
an ordering constraint as a prerequisite.** It is distinct from P10 S5 “reduce the visibility of
`pub` items” (Rust's `pub(crate)` narrowing).

**Why it must be completed in full before P4**: 029g's deletion surface overlaps with the rewrite
surfaces of the four refactoring phases. Reversing the order means repeating the same deletions and
baseline updates on the new grammar / checker layout / SSA IR.

| 029g deletion surface                                              | Files                                                                                                              | Rewritten by which phase                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `KwPub` and syntax branches                                        | `lexer/{tokens,state,mod}.rs`; `parser/parser_state.rs`, `statements/{declarations,imports,functions,bindings}.rs` | **P8** (8.2 lexical convergence / 8.3 Pratt precedence convergence / 8.7 split `parse_assign_after_target`)                                                                                                                                                                      |
| AST `is_pub` ×2 (`Assign` / `TypeDefinition`)                      | `parser/ast.rs`                                                                                                    | **P6 6.5 AST dead variants** + P8 + D1 directory renaming (`parser/ast.rs` → top-level `src/ast/`)                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `auto_bind_to_type` and the pub branches of `collect_exports`      | `typecheck/checker.rs`, `environment.rs`                                                                           | **P5** (`include!`→true `mod` and checker splitting are **pure moves**, and the pub branches would be moved unchanged into the new files)                                                                                                                                        |
| Dead-code exemption `exempt_pub` / `is_exported` and role dispatch | `typecheck/passes/dead_code.rs`, `module/orchestrator.rs` (inside `check_project`)                                 | **P4 4.2.2** (`check_project` slimming)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `semantic_tokens`'s `Public` modifier                              | `typecheck/checker/semantic_tokens.rs`                                                                             | **P4 4.2.6** + P5 + 4.6 (D55)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `ir_gen`'s ignoring of `is_pub` bindings                           | `middle/core/ir_gen.rs`                                                                                            | **P7** (`ir_gen.rs` SSA conversion)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| 31 occurrences of `pub` in `src/std/`                              | `std/list.yx` / `json.yx` / `option.yx` / `result.yx`                                                              | No phase modifies them directly, but **P6's hard acceptance criterion requires zero differences in `src/std/`** (`git diff --stat src/std/` is empty; the `tests/` direct-driver test exception is described in P6's hard-acceptance section)—therefore this must land before P6 |

**Incidental benefit**: 029g removes the dead `env.exports` table (filled by the producer and
consumed nowhere in the repository) and the `is_exported`/`is_visible` accessors ⇒ P4's 4.3
obligation ledger loses one more “produced but never consumed” field.

- [x] **029g.1 Lexer and parser branches**
- [x] **029g.2 AST `is_pub` ×2**
- [x] **029g.3 Checker side**
- [x] **029g.4 Dead-code exemption convergence**
- [x] **029g.5 Peripheral echo**
- [x] **029g.6 std and diagnostic text**
- [x] **029g.7 Language-reference convergence**

| Level 2                    | Level 3                                                                                                                    | Files                                                                                                                | Acceptance                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| -------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| 029g.1 Lexer and parser    | 029g.1.1 Delete the `KwPub` variant and the `keyword_from_str` entry                                                       | `lexer/tokens.rs`, `state.rs`, `mod.rs`                                                                              | The keyword table goes from 18→17 (`Kw*` 16→15, including the count comment in `tokens.rs`)                                                                                                                  |
|                            | 029g.1.2 Delete pub detection at declaration positions and `pre_detected_pub`                                              | `parser/statements/declarations.rs`, `functions.rs`, `bindings.rs`                                                   | `pub f = ...` reports a parse error (**red criterion first**)                                                                                                                                                |
|                            | 029g.1.3 Delete pub skipping within `use` brace entries and leading pub before statements                                  | `parser/statements/imports.rs`, `parser_state.rs`                                                                    | `pub use m` reports a parse error (**red criterion first**)                                                                                                                                                  |
| 029g.2 AST                 | 029g.2.1 Delete `is_pub` from `Assign`/`TypeDefinition` + synchronize construction sites                                   | `parser/ast.rs` and each construction site                                                                           | No pub remnants in the compiler (`grep -rn is_pub src/` is empty)                                                                                                                                            |
| 029g.3 Checker             | 029g.3.1 Delete `auto_bind_to_type` (pub was its only call reason)                                                         | `checker.rs`, `environment.rs`, call site `checker.rs`                                                               | The method form retains only explicit composition (RFC-004); zero dependencies in std or the corpus                                                                                                          |
|                            | 029g.3.2 Remove the pub branch from `collect_exports` + delete the dead `env.exports` table and `is_exported`/`is_visible` | `checker.rs`, `environment.rs`                                                                                       | The sole export authority = module registry `ModuleInfo.exports`; one fewer field in the 9.2 gate                                                                                                            |
| 029g.4 Dead-code exemption | 029g.4.1 Delete `exempt_pub` / `is_exported` and the exemption branch                                                      | `dead_code.rs`                                                                                                       | Unreferenced top-level bindings in Lib/Script roles report W1001 (**red criterion first**)                                                                                                                   |
|                            | 029g.4.2 Synchronize `orchestrator` role dispatch                                                                          | `orchestrator.rs`                                                                                                    | Single-file / multi-file diagnostic sets are normalized (C2); W codes are not mixed into consumers                                                                                                           |
| 029g.5 Peripheral echo     | 029g.5.1 Formatter pub prefix                                                                                              | `formatter/handlers/stmt.rs`                                                                                         | Formatting is idempotent (existing criterion)                                                                                                                                                                |
|                            | 029g.5.2 `semantic_tokens`'s `Public` modifier                                                                             | `typecheck/checker/semantic_tokens.rs`                                                                               | Semantic coloring no longer produces Public                                                                                                                                                                  |
| 029g.6 std and text        | 029g.6.1 Remove 31 pub prefixes from `src/std`                                                                             | `std/{list,json,option,result}.yx`                                                                                   | Corpus and std self-checks are green; **must be completed before P6**                                                                                                                                        |
|                            | 029g.6.2 Diagnostic text                                                                                                   | 4 W1xxx help messages                                                                                                | No longer claim that “pub is the public interface”; aligned with the Bin-role behavior in `dead_code.rs`                                                                                                     |
| 029g.7 Documentation       | 029g.7.1 Language reference and guides                                                                                     | `reference/language-spec/{modules,syntax,language-overview}.md`, `warning-code/warning-codes.md`, `guide/modules.md` | The specification no longer contains statements contradicting the implementation by saying that “pub can still be written”; the “pub never triggers W1001” statement in `warning-codes.md` is also corrected |

**Acceptance (behavior changes; red criterion first)**:

1. **Red before green**: each of the two behavior changes must first have a red criterion before
   code is changed—① `pub` changes from “silently accepted” to a parse error; ② unreferenced
   top-level bindings in Lib/Script roles newly report W1001 (previously pub items were always
   exempt).
2. **Test count must not decrease**: when deleting the `exempt_pub` case and `is_pub` fixture from
   `typecheck/passes/tests/dead_code.rs`, they must be replaced with equivalent new cases.
3. **Baseline in the same commit**: the diagnostic-set change (new parse error + new W1001) requires
   the corpus differential baseline (`tests/baselines/corpus-parity.jsonl`) and CHANGELOG to be
   updated in the same commit; IR snapshots are expected to have zero differences, and any nonzero
   difference requires an explanation.
4. **Independent revert unit**: the entire batch occupies one commit group and must not be mixed
   with other phases (DoD 6).
5. `cargo test` / `clippy -D warnings` / `python scripts/rfc/check_tracking.py` are all green.

**Ordering**: It overlaps with 3.4 at the file level (`checker.rs`, `environment.rs`)⇒ the two must
be serial and cannot run in parallel; **the whole must precede P4** (RFC-039 D56). The 029g track
does not depend on 3.4; implementers may choose which to begin based on the context at hand.

> **Commit comparison (2026-10-08)**: This section's incorporation (the P3.5 section +
> main-chain/constraint rows) was merged into `97071c81b` while the parallel workstream was cleaning
> the workspace; that commit's message records “3.4 supplementary registration + D57/D58 decision
> filing” and does not mention this track. History is not rewritten; following the existing
> convention (the `d5b18390` precedent, see the P2 landing note), it is registered in place as
> evidence for retrospective bisection.

|                                                       | 4.4.4 The shared-solver observation ledger `ConsumerStats`/`CONSUMER_STATS`: every production point writes, while the production side has zero reads (the only read point is `proof/smt/tests/shared.rs`). **Decision ① of 2026-10-24: retain it**—write “the sole consumer = tests” as a documentation contract (preserving the D58 observable-criterion carrier); Decision ② “delete it” is not taken (the 4.4.2 acceptance criterion would have to be rewritten at the same time, and the trade-off rationale is in the 4.4 closeout “sixteen”). **Terminal state**: the three contract sentences are written into the module header of `src/frontend/core/typecheck/proof/smt/backend.rs` and the relevant item documentation comments—① the write surface = every acquisition through the shared interface increments one atomic counter; ② the read surface is **only** `proof/smt/tests/shared.rs` (zero calls from production code); ③ `SolverConsumer::ALL` is a **test-only constant** and production code must not depend on it; **zero behavior change** (only comments are changed, and the 4.4.2 acceptance numbers remain unchanged; issue [#475](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/475)) |                                                       | 4.9.9 A cross-unit single-definition method loses its formal-name table when called with named arguments (registered 2026-11-25; discovered during the 7c closeout): calling a cross-unit method (injected by the registry) with named arguments reports `E1014 Function '…' has no parameter named '…' (available: )`—the name table is empty; positional calls to the same method work normally, as do named-argument calls within a single file. Minimal reproduction: `lib.yx` defines `Point.scale_by: (self: &Point, k: Float) -> Float`, and `main.yx` contains `p.scale_by(k = 2.0)` ⇒ E1014, while `p.scale_by(2.0)` ⇒ 6.0. **D59 classification: category C** (old behavior was not success: named arguments were silently dropped, followed by runtime failure or silent wrong value). **Scope (final form after two rounds of narrowing on 2026-11-26—add only three items; everything else is deletion and registration)**: ① `AstToIrGenerator::method_param_info` (`src/middle/lower/query.rs`)—obtains the declaration-time formal names from the sole cross-unit carrier `ModuleInfo::method_overloads` by the defining unit's qualified name `{module}.{Type}.{method}[#N]` (the candidate ↔ mixed-IR-name ↔ formal-name vectors are in the same order, using the same source as the typecheck injection surface); the call site (`src/middle/lower/expr/call.rs`) uses `method_dispatch_name`'s qualified name to look up the name table, rather than two bare-name segments. These two locations fix only cross-unit methods using **bare-name imports** (`local == target`): the candidate-injection surface retains the pre-4.9.9 narrowing rule (`local != target` is skipped), so named arguments on alias imports are outside the fix scope (see “Known Gaps”). ② `ModuleRegistry::all_method_bindings` (`src/frontend/module/registry.rs`) merges in **ascending module-key order**—**category C (independent of the original bug): eliminate “the same input produces multiple results” caused by HashMap iteration order**, rather than fixing the original bug; criterion = same-file unit test `test_all_method_bindings_merge_is_deterministic_across_hash_seeds` (32 registry instances with alternating insertion orders; only one single binding is permitted per method key; removing the ascending-order merge produces two results). **Criterion (red before green)**: 2 driver-level tests in `src/driver/tests/named_args_cross_unit_method.rs` (bare-import single candidate 6.0 / two candidates selected by name to 6.0 and 30, both with value assertions; the file header marks the medical-history gate); unit test in `src/middle/lower/tests/query.rs` (`method_param_info` empty-name-table guard ⇒ None, positive control returns a non-empty table unchanged); unit test in `src/frontend/module/registry.rs` (the deterministic criterion above). Project fixture `tests/yaoxiang-multifile/named-args-cross-unit-method` (the `alias-overload` fixture is deleted along with the alias surface's retirement); the corpus differential baseline `tests/baselines/corpus-parity.jsonl` now has 404 lines: **relative to the direct parent commit `ac810e97`, 405→404** (an intermediate state once included `alias-overload`); **relative to the pre-4.9.9 baseline `08ccacc5`, 403→404** (+1 basic fixture `named-args-cross-unit-method`; `alias-overload` never entered the final history). The line-count change = the inevitable result of fixtures entering and leaving (the regenerated `corpus_probe` is byte-for-byte identical to the stored baseline), **not lost coverage**. **Known gaps (each named explicitly; old behavior = silently ignored or silently dropped, not fixed; D60② should have been reported but was not, category A)**: closure-value binding / block-body function declaration / named nested function declaration / built-in container constructors other than `len` with named arguments / namespace arm / ordinary static-function arm / **alias-import named arguments** (candidate injection recognizes only `local == target` ⇒ alias-import candidates are not injected, the IR has no resolution entry, and named arguments are silently dropped; the minimal form `use a.{Temp as X}` + `t.bump(k = 2.0)` produces runtime E6007 both before 4.9.9 and after narrowing; the positional form `t.bump(2.0)` in the same shape works normally; an intermediate implementation before narrowing made it work by injecting alias candidates, but that mechanism was rolled back in this round) / whole-module import / RFC-004 binding arm. There are also two residual identity surfaces (not fixed; migration surfaces are listed in 4.9.10): a same-name type owned by this unit + an alias import (two defining-side identities with the same name coexist, and the import surface cannot register the unit's own declaration); function-body-level / cross-scope imports are merged at whole-unit granularity (a shared import table)—that shape itself does not work (an existing defect: check passes, run reports E6006), was not introduced by this batch, and remains unfixed. **Deletions in this round (relative to the first six rounds of 4.9.9)**: candidate-provider priority, candidate-signature comparison, owner/compatibility inference, the `frontend::module::import_face` shared-provider table, the IR-side “no name table ⇒ E1014” fallback, and the **E5006 ambiguity prohibition at import-name resolution**—the latter rejects mixed/alias-only forms that previously ran on the parent commit (category D); this was withdrawn by the decision of 2026-11-26; this round introduces no new ambiguity determination. **Visible changes**: ① cross-unit method named arguments (**bare-name imports**, including two-candidate overloads) change from an E1014 empty table to binding by name into slots (measured 6.0 / 30); ② all other forms return to pre-4.9.9 behavior (named arguments silently ignored or dropped; see “Known Gaps”); ③ the single method binding for the same input no longer varies with HashMap iteration order (category C). **Status**: completed on 2026-11-26 (including the narrowing round and review pass). | 4.9.7         | Criteria are in `src/driver/tests/named_args_cross_unit_method.rs`, `src/middle/lower/tests/query.rs`, and `src/frontend/module/registry.rs`; root-cause treatment is in 4.9.10                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
|                                                       | 4.9.10 Cross-unit method identity is undefined (registered 2026-11-25; structural issue confirmed during 4.9.9 implementation): the method-candidate key `{Type}.{method}` does not contain a module qualification, so for same-name types across modules, three consumers—candidate injection (`driver::arms::visible_method_overloads`), typecheck's formal-name/candidate matching, and IR dispatch (`method_dispatch_name`)—each guess the provider by name, and changing any one of them moves the “silent wrong value” elsewhere. **Root-cause solution**: upgrade type identity to a **module-qualified identity** (`{module}.{Type}`), and change the method-candidate key accordingly to `{module}.{Type}.{method}`; one resolver produces it, and the three consumers only read it. This requires changing the shape of the `overload_resolutions` key and mixed-IR names; D59 **category D** (requires a decision). **Disposition in this round (the criterion after two narrowing rounds of 4.9.9)**: 4.9.9 **introduces no new rejection**—the prior six rounds' category-D rejection list (all named arguments produce E1014 / conflicting positional forms all produce E1107 / the E5006 ambiguity prohibition at import-name resolution) is deleted and entirely invalidated; import forms and positional arguments for mixed / alias-only / conflicting forms that ran on the parent commit become runnable again; **named arguments on alias imports remain silently dropped** (`use a.{Temp as X}` + `t.bump(k = 2.0)` ⇒ runtime E6007), unfixed; see 4.9.9 “Known Gaps.” **Residual surfaces (to be closed during migration)**: ① candidate/method keys still lack module qualification; ② a same-name type owned by this unit + an alias import (two defining-side identities with the same name coexist); ③ function-body-level / cross-scope imports are merged at whole-unit granularity (a shared import table), and that shape itself does not work (an existing defect: check passes, run reports E6006); ④ named arguments are still silently ignored or dropped on closure-value binding / block-body function declaration / named nested function declaration / built-in container constructors other than `len` / namespace arm / ordinary static-function arm / alias-import named arguments / whole-module import / RFC-004 binding arm (D60② category A). **Status**: pending scheduling (category D, requires a decision). ### P5 Split the checker Within the File — Supplied by This Document (Original Gap G1)

Source: RFC definition + `01-routing.md` directory tree + hard constraints (the `pub(crate)` path of
`collect_used_in_type` must not change)

- [x] **5.1 `include!` → true `mod`** (2 level-3 tasks)
- [x] **5.2 Extract `refinement`** (2 level-3 tasks)
- [x] **5.3 Extract `annotations`** (2 level-3 tasks)

| Level 2                     | Level 3                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Prerequisite | Acceptance                                                                                                                                |
| --------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 5.1 `include!` → true `mod` | 5.1.1 Change the `include!` at the end of `checker.rs` to a true `mod` declaration (do not hard-code the line anchor; measurement at the time placed it at the end of the file)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | P4 complete  | C1 zero-diff; `semantic_tokens.rs` gains module identity                                                                                  |
|                             | 5.1.2 Add `use super::*` or individual imports (`include!` implicitly inherited the scope)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | 5.1.1        | `cargo build` is green                                                                                                                    |
| 5.2 Extract `refinement`    | 5.2.1 Move out the refinement blocks (the three noncontiguous sections formerly in `checker.rs`; now a single `checker/refinement.rs` file containing `ReturnRefinement` / `RefinedWalkCtx`), leaving the shared surface and the 6 cross-boundary shared items in place                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | 5.1.1        | C1 zero-diff                                                                                                                              |
|                             | 5.2.2 Consolidate the refinement entry point into “`&self` + return diagnostics,” **without leaving impl**: `collect_refined_binding_checks` (now `checker/refinement.rs`, `pub(super)`) no longer implicitly writes to the error channel through `&mut self`; the diagnostic set is returned upward, and the sole write point is the sole caller `check_module_impl` (`checker.rs`, symbol location). **Reason for remaining in impl**: the only field used by this family is `self.env` (leaving impl is technically possible), but converting the entry point and traversal entirely into free functions would also move these call sites: `resolve_type_annotation` has 8 production sites (6 annotation paths + `refined_walk_stmt` + `collect_refined_var_names_in_stmt`), `collect_param_refinements` has 2 production sites + 1 test, and it would create a reverse dependency from the “retained surface (signature collection) → refinement,” contrary to this step's goal of eliminating coupling | 5.2.1        | —                                                                                                                                         |
| 5.3 Extract `annotations`   | 5.3.1 Move out the 10-item annotation-validation family (now a single `checker/annotations.rs` file; `check_annotation_type_names` is the `pub(super)` entry point, the other 9 items are private to the family, and `is_predicate_head` is a free function within the family)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | 5.2.2        | C1 zero-diff                                                                                                                              |
|                             | 5.3.2 **`collect_used_in_type` (`pub(crate)`) remains `pub(crate)` and under the `checker` module path**: it is a **shared-surface** function (consumed by both `collect_function_signature` and `inference/statements.rs:16`), and 5.3.1 **did not migrate it** according to measurement ⇒ the “zero caller changes” criterion **was already met**, so **do not add** a new `pub(crate) use annotations::*;` re-export. **Retained constraint**: if it is ever migrated with the family (into annotations or another later batch), `checker.rs` must add `pub(crate) use annotations::*;` so that the import remains character-for-character unchanged                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | 5.3.1        | **Zero caller changes** is this step's hard acceptance criterion: `git diff src/frontend/core/typecheck/inference/statements.rs` is empty |

**Hard constraint**: `collect_used_in_type` is used across modules by `inference/statements.rs:16`.
**If** the function is ever migrated with the annotation family, **then** `checker.rs` must add
`pub(crate) use annotations::*;` so that the import remains character-for-character unchanged;
measurement shows that 5.3 did **not** migrate it, so this batch adds no re-export (the criterion
was already met).

**Only split confirmed large blocks.** Do not split modules of only 200–400 lines—that would
increase navigation cost, reduce cohesion, and provide zero decoupling benefit. The remaining
submodules (signatures / type_defs / imports) will be split after their files grow back to 1,500
lines.

**6.9 Model and Invariants (First-Principles Rewrite, 2026-11-13)**

> **Model**: D62's end state is a **unified item world**—type positions and value positions share
> one set of compile-time items. One strength declaration produces one quantifier reading
> `q ∈ {0,1,ω}`; `q` is the **sole input** to slot allocation, and the call-site actual-argument
> view and implementation-header binding view are two projections of the same fact. Equality is a
> **pure decision** (identical after normalizing both sides; does not bind type variables); evidence
> is the **sole entry point** for q=0 slots (four sources: literals / value environment /
> single-value pinning / Γ equalities); erasure is the **runtime consequence** of q=0 (closed terms
> and open terms/recursion both hold); Σ construction points **reuse the same evidence interface**
> rather than establishing another one. **The completion criterion = the invariants hold, not the
> mere completion of subphase names.**

> **6.9 closeout criterion**: I1–I5 and I7 all hold; I6 is closed according to the design-review
> conclusion for 6.9.5 (does not block P7); I8 has evidence for every slice; residual items
> #537/#539/#540/#541/#542/#552 all have a disposition (fix / explicit exclusion / separate case);
> #542 landed on 2026-11-19. **Do not substitute “all subphases checked off” for the invariants
> holding.**

> **Partial order (not a serial chain)**: A facts → {A.C3, B.d, C.2, D design}; B kernel → {B.d,
> C.2, D implementation}; C.1 is independent and complete; F4 decision → A.C3; decision-point
> protocol → B.d; design review → D implementation. A.C3 and B.d may run in parallel but require
> file-surface coordination (C3 leans typecheck→mono, while d leans types/solver→sema); C.2 may run
> in parallel with B.d after 6.9.2-B/6.9.3a (it does not depend on defeq); D's implementation waits
> for confirmation of the B design package; it depends on A (q=0 erasure) and C.1 (dispatch), and
> may depend on 6.9.3's typed exit. **P7 gate = A/B/C exits; D does not block.**

> **A.C3 prerequisite: F4 decision (A decided on 2026-11-16; measurement)**: The old wording
> “`CheckOnly` evidence does not enter the request” is obsolete—`ConstEvidence`/`CheckOnly` have
> been deleted; the current mechanism is that an open term's `normalize` returns `Unproven` ⇒ no
> request is created, and the representation layer `GenericArg::Const(ConstExpr)` can already hold
> `k-1`. **The decision was changed to two orthogonal decisions + one termination subdecision**: D-a
> representation (A: open terms enter `deferred` and are normalized before enqueueing / B: do not
> specialize); D-b evaluation point (only mono; C-1 second field and C-2 restoration from IR both
> break the single representation/evaluator, so C-3 = A); **F4-T termination** (naive firing pushes
> the recursive chain into `max_depth` and hits E3005; T-a apply the condition within the instance /
> T-b bounded firing + fallback to the original at the boundary / T-c add a termination layer or
> change the specification). **Do it first, then decide; before measurement, do not treat “A is
> already implementable” as a conclusion.** **User (2026-11-16): D-a=A-closed, D-b=C-3, F4-T=T-b,
> bound=8; C3 implementation is a separate task and does not claim complete I4.**

> **B.d prerequisite: decision-point protocol (not yet documented)**: RFC-027 §12.1 stipulates that
> “the decision point first follows the unified path; after the unified path fails, defeq may be
> used, but any partial bindings produced by the unification attempt must first be rolled back.” The
> implementation must answer: is the entry point an outer wrapper around `solver.unify` or a new
> `decide` layer; when may defeq be attempted (only when both sides are ground, with no unbound
> variables); what are the snapshot/restore boundary and cost; and whether unify and defeq share one
> budget or have separate budgets. **Responsibility boundary**: unify remains responsible for
> subtyping/Any/Never; defeq only decides equality and does not take over subtyping; bidirectional
> subtyping must not be treated as equality (RFC-027 §12.1). **Provide the protocol and production
> call graph before writing code**; 6.9.3c's wiring contract has already wasted two rounds because
> it assumed `can_assign` was the decision point, while `AssignmentChecker` has zero production
> instantiations.

> **E acceptance and residuals (I8, cross-cutting)**: Every slice must provide ① zero
> corpus/IR-snapshot drift or item-by-item classification; ② `testsuite run fast` + affected
> integration tests; ③ idempotence of the error-code generator (if an emission point/code table
> changes); ④ classification of behavior changes under D59 and red before green. **Wiring tasks
> additionally require**: production call-graph evidence (which production entry point, how many
> paths, which path is reachable); single-test reachability must not be passed off as user
> reachability; rollback and budget require measured numbers. **Residual disposition**: #537
> (category D, proof-function formal-parameter refinement silently discarded; do not fix before the
> decision, and 6.9.4/6.9.5 must not depend on its fix), #539 (import-surface q=0; a category-C
> stage boundary; if it remains open, 6.9.4 covers only module-local behavior), #540 (deeply nested
> stack overflow; **closed**—3.20.30 closed on 2026-11-14: 64MB large-stack thread in the
> orchestration layer + parser depth gate MAX_PARSE_DEPTH=1024 + E0019; see the 3.20.30 row in 09),
> #541 (TailCall; **closed**—three sites changed together on 2026-11-16: `call_form_is_rewritten` /
> `replace_calls_in_function` / `drop_erased_call_args` cover `Call`/`TailCall` through the single
> point `rewritten_call_target`; init sections and function bodies share one site-rewriting source;
> red→green + three mutations), #542 (orphaned shared layer; **landed (2026-11-19)**: DCE pass +
> post-mono two entry points wired + C2c temporary avoidance removed; red→green, 402/402,
> function-table attribution and deviations), #552 (imported multi-parameter predicate proof
> execution; a neighboring area to 6.9.5, handled separately). **Ownership pending decision**:
> `rfc027_refined_transparency`'s four open-constraint tripwires (`Eq(N, n)`) do not belong to
> 6.9.4's constant-equality bridge; decide whether they remain in 6.9.4's outOfScope or move to
> 6.9.1/E4018's proof-function surface.

> **Non-claims / boundaries**: This item covers record Σ (B: witness field) only, does not touch
> Π/`DepFn`, and does not claim a complete Π/Σ implementation; 6.9.4 covers only constant equalities
> and does not fulfill the open-constraint `Eq(N,n)` tripwire; `f(y)(y)`'s second value position is
> outside this item; witness erasure ≠ Runtime check erasure; if #537 is not fixed, 6.9.4/6.9.5
> cover only forms that do not depend on proof-function formal-parameter refinement; if #539 is not
> fixed, 6.9.4 covers only module-local behavior; #540 is closed (3.20.30); #542 has landed
> (2026-11-19); 2.1.4's inner-isolation red state was cleared on 2026-11-20 (the criterion was
> rewritten according to slot identity in the 2.1.4 closeout)⇒ all prerequisites for promoting
> `verify` to a gate are removed.

**Hard acceptance** (**criteria revised on 2026-10-27, following the 2026-10-26 decision ②**):
`git diff --stat tests/yaoxiang tests/baselines src/std/` must be empty **in every round of
6.1–6.7**. **Direct-driver test files** under `tests/` (tests that directly drive modified code,
such as `tests/formatter_integration.rs`) may be modified when **mechanically required by the
change**, but each modification must be registered with **file / line count / reason**; **tests must
not be deleted and assertions must not be weakened**. **Exception**: 6.8.2 is a **behavioral-fix**
batch (new diagnostic code + baseline update), does not fall under this hard acceptance criterion,
is isolated into a separate round, and requires a separate decision.

> **Revision relationship**: The original criterion of 2026-10-26 decision ② was written as
> `git diff --stat tests/ src/std/`, applying a blanket zero-difference requirement to all of
> `tests/`; the revised criterion changes only direct-driver test files from “zero differences” to
> “may be changed but must register file / line count / reason”. **The zero-difference requirements
> for the corpus layer (`tests/yaoxiang`), baseline layer (`tests/baselines`), and standard library
> (`src/std/`) remain unchanged.** First application = 6.1.2: only `tests/formatter_integration.rs`
> changed by **3 lines** (2 replacements of `i64`→`Int64` in `assert_format_eq` assertions + 1
> comment line), with the reason = the expected type name in the assertion must be synchronized with
> the changed spelling; no tests were deleted and no assertions were weakened. See “P6 6.1 Name
> Canonicalization Closeout.”

### P7 Convert the Intermediate Representation to SSA

Source: [04](04-ssa.md) §Implementation Points Batches a–d, criterion C4, **strictly serial within a
batch**.

- [x] **7.0 Pure-move split** (6 level-3 tasks)

> **7.0 design basis and boundaries**: The target layout comes from the directory tree and migration
> table in [01-routing](01-routing.md)—`middle/ir/ir_gen.rs` → `middle/lower/`,
> `sema/inference/expressions.rs` → `sema/infer/` (call-resolution family `call/`). This batch
> **only performs pure moves**, with the acceptance criterion of “zero semantic diff apart from
> paths” (manual review of every file) + C1 snapshot zero-diff. It does not conflict with RFC-039
> [RFC-039](../../rfc/accepted/039-compiler-architecture.md) alternative A's rejection of “split
> files only, without changing the paradigm”: what was rejected is using moves **in place of**
> paradigm work, while this batch is the **prerequisite** for paradigm work—7c.1's `lower/synth.rs`
> depends on the `lower/` directory being in place, and 7d.1's `generate_call_expr_ir` method split
> is performed on smaller files after the move. The expressions side follows the decision (split
> only the call-resolution family; `statements.rs` remains in place); the whole
> `inference/ → infer/` rename and merging statements belong to the full migration, are outside this
> batch, and require a separate design round after P7. `synth.rs` is a new boundary rather than a
> move and belongs to 7c.1. Survey baseline (2026-11-12): `ir_gen.rs` has 9,804 lines and 148
> methods, with the body consisting of a single `impl AstToIrGenerator` block (about 9,250 lines);
> `expressions.rs` has 6,825 lines and 93 methods, with the body consisting of a single
> `impl ExpressionInferrer` block (about 6,280 lines); the largest methods are `infer_call_expr` at
> 1,671 lines and `generate_call_expr_ir` at 866 lines (the latter is the target of 7d.1).

- [x] **7a Cut multiple definitions**
- [x] **7b Switch to the SSA form** (5 level-3 tasks)

> **Retention condition for A/I4 (rewritten 2026-11-13)**: 6.9.2-C2c's “conservatively retain the
> original” was a temporary form while I4 was incomplete—the original still carried q=0 slots, while
> the clone materialized body reads as constants. This avoidance was removed with #542's landing on
> 2026-11-19: mono no longer deletes originals by generic name, the restoration gate is removed, and
> deletion responsibility moves to post-mono DCE; the remaining “original is not erased” behavior is
> C3 T-b's long-term fallback-to-original semantics at the boundary, not an avoidance.
> **Reevaluation after C3 (2026-11-16)**: C3's T-b fallback to the original at the boundary leaves
> the call site of the “9th firing” on the original ⇒ the original is put back and the shared layer
> still has referents, so #542's surface is narrowed to the “non-recursive fully covered” form
> (`fn_const_generic` family); this was landed on 2026-11-19 (post-mono DCE wiring, removing this

**Risk**: The precedence table is the sole authority, and missing a consumer silently changes
associativity (no error, only a change in parse result). The defenses are D1–D3 permanent assertions
and operator-property cross-checking tests; every new operator must simultaneously enter the table
and the cross-check test.

> **8.3–8.6 depend on D0/D2 from `08`**: if the grammar file is placed outside `parser/`, or action
> code refers backward to `sema`, it will be blocked by `check-boundary.py`.

### P9 Anti-Rebound Gates (Unified Script Inventory — This Document Supplies Original Gap G3)

Source: [01](01-routing.md) §Anti-Rebound Mechanism + [08](08-maintenance-mechanism.md)
§Machine-Checkable Rules

**This is the sole authoritative inventory of all CI scripts** (10 active scripts; the 9.8 row is
retained as a cancellation registration). Scripts are connected to CI at their “introduction phase”;
those marked report-only are converted to hard failures uniformly in P9 (changing from “report” to
“fail”)—existing violations (parallel representations, disambiguation aliases, etc.) are not cleared
until P6/P8, and converting to hard failure early would leave CI permanently red and cause gates to
be bypassed manually; when P9 converts them to hard failures, the gates should be exactly green.
P2's 2.5, P4's 4.3.2, and P7's 7c.1 all refer to this table and establish no separate inventory.

## Implementation Points: Dependencies and Parallelization

### Mandatory Serial Main Chain

`P0 → P1 → P2 → P3 → P3.5 → P4 → P5 → P6 → (P7 ∥ P8) → P9 → P10`

> **P3 stop-the-bleed path (decision of 2026-10-05)**: P3's prerequisites are narrowed to **2.3.2 +
> 2.4.1 + 2.4.3** (multi-file corpus layer + two red criteria). The rest of P2 (2.1 IR verifier, 2.2
> snapshots, 2.3.1/2.3.3 differential and performance baseline) can proceed in parallel with P3.
> Stopping correctness vulnerabilities is not blocked by criterion-infrastructure construction;
> however, **P4 must still wait for all of P2**—the unified Driver requires all three layers of
> criteria to be in place.

| Constraint                | Reason                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| ------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| P0 → P1                   | The three prohibitions constrain every action in P1 (1.2.x is precisely about adding `mod` declarations to `mod.rs`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| P1 → P2                   | Reviving tests changes the test count and corpus baseline                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| P2 (after narrowing) → P3 | Vulnerability criteria must **be written as red first** (2.4.1/2.4.3) + the multi-file corpus layer (2.3.2) must exist so that the red tests have somewhere to run                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| P3 → P4                   | Fix bugs before refactoring; reversing the order would cause bugs to be fixed into “established behavior” by the phase table                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| P3.5 → P4                 | RFC-029g (#399)'s deletion surface covers P4 4.2.2 / P5 / P6 / P7 / P8's respective rewrite targets (the inventory is in §P3.5 of this document)—reversing the order means repeating the same deletions on the new grammar, new checker layout, and new IR (D56)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| P4 → P5                   | The `include!` refactor and checker split modify the same file                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| P5 → P6                   | Consecutive changes to the same file must be separated; otherwise, regressions cannot be localized by bisection                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| P6 → P7 / P8              | If type representations do not converge first, the new IR will grow into a third representation; **the P7 gate = the phase A/B/C exits of 6.9** (I1–I3, I5, I7; namely 6.9.2 including C3, 6.9.3 including d, and completed 6.9.4), while D (Σ) does not block; P7 may consume quantifier-derived views and the defeq interface, but must not create a parallel ledger; the 7.0 pure-move batch is a P7 prerequisite, is not subject to this gate, and must not consume the undecided defeq/quantifier interfaces |
| P7/P8 → P9                | The 9.5 snapshot baseline and the 9.3 `pub(crate)` leak count take their final form only after P7/P8                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |

### Parallelizable Groups

| Group                                        | Members                                                                            | Basis                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| -------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **A (the only group authorized by the RFC)** | P7 ∥ P8                                                                            | Disjoint file sets: `ir.rs`/`ir_gen.rs`/`bytecode.rs`/`translator.rs` vs `lexer/*`/`parser/*`                                                                                                                                                                             |
| B                                            | 1.2.1 ∥ 1.2.2 ∥ 1.2.3 ∥ 1.2.4                                                      | Each independently adds `mod`; revert file by file                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| C                                            | 2.1 ∥ 2.2 ∥ 2.3.1 ∥ 2.3.2 ∥ 2.4                                                    | The three layers of criteria are independent                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| D                                            | 4.4 (proof-layer cleanup) ∥ the entire P6 line                                     | `02` explicitly states in S5 that it “should proceed independently of S1-S4”                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| E                                            | 4.2.5 ∥ 4.2.6 ∥ 4.2.8                                                              | All are mutually independent after 4.1                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| F                                            | 9.3 ∥ 9.9 ∥ 9.10 (the three scripts introduced in P0 can be developed in parallel) | The 9.5 snapshot baseline must be taken last                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| G                                            | S3 ∥ S4 ∥ S5                                                                       | Disjoint file sets (verified on 2026-10-05: S3 modifies `docs/src/dev/design/check/` + `config.js`; S4 modifies `docs/src/rfc/` + the `check_tracking.py` generator; S5 modifies 2 `pub` items under `src/frontend/core/typecheck/`; there are no pairwise intersections) |

**Cannot run in parallel (file overlap)**: P1(1.1.x) ∥ P8(8.0.x); 1.2.1 ∥ 8.3.2; 6.0 ∥ S2 (B6/E5 in
the same file batch); 4.3.2 ∥ 9.2 (now unified under P4); **3.4 ∥ P3.5** (`checker.rs` /
`environment.rs` are the same files—the external track's pub-deletion batch and the silent-path
fixes must be serial).

**New internal documentation constraint**: 6.0→6.1→6.2→6.3→6.4→6.5→6.6 is strictly serial; 7.0 →
7a→7b→7c→7d is strictly serial (7.0 is P7's pure-move prerequisite batch), and 7b's `Phi` arm must
precede 7d.

## Key Decisions and Rationale

- **P0 is an independent phase rather than part of P9**—gates are “post-hoc checks,” while rules are
  “pre-hoc judgment.” P9's scripts cannot prevent “patching when a refactoring is required”; only
  the D0–D4 review checklist can.
- **P9 is the sole authoritative inventory of CI scripts**—the original gap G3's three concurrent
  inventories made closure impossible. This table merges them so that the 10 active scripts each
  belong to one phase (9.8 was cancelled by the user on 2026-11-24), with report-only status and
  hard-conversion timing explicitly traceable.
- **P3.5 incorporates RFC-029g rather than leaving it as an “independent track to execute after the
  refactoring”**—its deletion surface overlaps with the rewrite surfaces of P4/P5/P6/P7/P8 (RFC-039
  D56). Deferring it until after the refactoring means repeating the same deletions on the new
  grammar, new checker layout, and new IR; this is the same lesson as incorporating 7e's
  `2315`/`2341` and S4's RFC-status corrections.
- **P5's steps are supplied by this document rather than merged into P4**—P4 is already the largest
  single-point risk, and adding the checker split would exceed the rollback granularity.
- **5.3.2 uses “zero caller changes” as hard acceptance**—the split must be independently
  revertable.

## Known Limitations and Risks

- **1.3.x (fixing exposed defects) has no regression criterion**—it only says “the fixed defects
  must not be rolled back” and lacks a record requirement identifying “what was fixed.” **Execution
  convention**: each 1.3.x fix's commit message must cite the fixed test name and defect description
  as evidence for retrospective bisection.
- ~~**7b.1 granularity is too coarse**~~ **Fixed (2026-10-05)**: split into the two exclusive
  commits 7b.1a / 7b.1b according to DoD 6.
- **8.2.1 granularity is too coarse**—about 89 reference-renaming sites; a missed change **silently
  alters associativity**.
- ~~**D17 / D20 have no corresponding level-3 tasks**~~ **Fixed (2026-10-05)**: D17 landed as 7e.1,
  and D20 landed as 4.5.1/4.5.2; D32/D34's opcode reclamation was merged into 7e.1, and the deletion
  action remains in P10 S6.
- ~~**The `2315` / `2341` data-loss defects were originally intended as an “independent issue”**~~
  **Fixed (2026-10-05)**: incorporated as 7e.2 / 7e.3. The exception table (throw/try) is core
  language semantics and must not be left for implementation.
- ~~**E2 (`lib.rs:568/582` `todo!()`) has no phase ownership**~~ **Fixed (2026-10-05)**: merged into
  P10 S2.
- ~~**F7 (opcode gate extractor) has ambiguous ownership**~~ **Fixed (2026-10-05)**: merged into the
  generation-time gate task in P10 S6.

## Conflict Register: All Decisions Made

> **The original “Conflict Register” C1–C8 and all open questions in the individual documents have
> converged into the [RFC-039 Decision Register](../../rfc/accepted/039-compiler-architecture.md)
> (D1–D50).** This document retains no pending items. The one-to-one correspondence is:

| Original number | Issue                                                                               | Decision                                                                                                                                                                        |
| --------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| C1              | `precedence_inline.rs` is revived in P1 and deleted in P8                           | **Revive** (P1's prerequisite is reviving tests with real assertions); P8 no longer deletes the file (Pratt is retained)                                                        |
| C2              | “Grammar-driven” commitment vs table-driven implementation                          | **Fallback approach**: retain Pratt + a single declarative precedence table + source-shared cross-checking of the two `BinOp` sets (the LALRPOP route is withdrawn); see P8 8.3 |
| C3              | Conflict between the C6 category definition and S1 classification                   | **S1 is classified under C6** (revival does not require equivalence criteria, only regression tests); C6 is expanded to “pure deletion or pure revival”                         |
| C4              | `test_release_plan_spans_consumed` allowlist                                        | **No allowlist**; the difference set must be empty; if it cannot be empty, the `ReleasePlan` contract is defective ⇒ change it to `PlanId` (D20/D41)                            |
| C5              | Internal differences between the two `Aggregation` functions have not been verified | **P4's 4.2.7 lists verification as mandatory**, with line-by-line confirmation during implementation                                                                            |
| C6              | The wasm playground fix requires P4's `ProgramKind`                                 | **The wasm-path vulnerability fix is merged into 4.2.8**; P3 handles only the three native entry points                                                                         |
| C7              | Ownership of `method_def_ordinals`                                                  | **Assigned to 04** (P7 batch c), see D21                                                                                                                                        |
| C8              | `upvalue_count` correction and the `.42` version number                             | **Fix + change `VERSION` from 4 to 5** (the format header already has `MAGIC` + `VERSION` fields, `codegen/bytecode.rs:14-16`), see D17                                         |
